Data Sharing Agreement 
Between 
the Navajo County Public Health Services District/Navajo County Medical Examiner 
and the Arizona Board of Regents for and on behalf of 
Arizona State University 
1. TERM OF THE AGREEMENT: 
The term of this Agreement shall become effective upon signature and shall remain in effect 
indefinitely unless terminated, canceled or extended as otherwise provided herein. 
2. TERMINATION OR AMENDMENT: 
Each party shall have the right to terminate this Agreement by mailing the other party written notice 
of termination by certified mail, return receipt requested, at least thirty (30) days prior to the 
termination date. This Agreement may be modified at any time by mutual written Amendment in 
order to accommodate unforeseen circumstances by the authorized representative of the 
respective parties. 
3. CONFIDENTIALITY: 
A. Any information that may be exchanged through this Agreement shall not be used for purposes 
other than those covered in the scope of work without prior approval of all parties to this 
Agreement. 
B. Neither confidential medical information nor personally identifying information that may be 
exchanged through this Agreement shall be made available for any political or commercial purpose, 
nor shall such information be used as basis for determining eligibility for care or source of payment 
for care to any individual. 
4. HEALTH INSURANCE PORTABILITY AND ACCOUNTABILITY ACT (HIPAA) COMPLIANCE: 
A. All parties agree to adhere to the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act of 1996 
(HIPAA) Privacy and Security Rules as defined in 45 CFR Parts 160, and 164 and under the HIS 
Circular No. 2003-02 for policy and procedures. 
B. Parties warrant that they will cooperate with the State in the course of performance of the 
Agreement so that both parties will be in compliance with HIPAA, including cooperation and 
coordination with State privacy officials and other compliance officers required by HIPAA and its 
regulations. Parties will participate in any training that shall be required or shall sign any 
documents that are reasonably necessary to keep both parties in compliance with HIPAA, including 
but not limited to business associate agreements, pledge of confidentiality, HIPAA training 
certification or other HIPAA related compliance documents.

<!-- page 165 -->
5. APPLICABLE LAW: 
Arizona Law. Applicable law of Arizona applies to this Agreement including, where applicable, the 
Uniform Commercial Code as adopted by the State of Arizona. 
6. CONFLICT OF INTEREST: 
Pursuant to AR.S. §38-511, the State, its political subdivisions or any department or agency of either 
may, within four (4) years after its execution, cancel any Agreement, without penalty or further 
obligation, made by the State, its political subdivisions, or any of the departments or agencies of 
either if any person significantly involved in initiating, negotiating, securing, drafting or creating the 
Agreement on behalf of the State, its political subdivisions or any of the departments or agencies of 
either is, at any time while the Agreement or any extension of the Agreement is in effect, an employee 
or agent of any other party to the Agreement in any capacity or a consultant to any other party of the 
Agreement with respect to the subject matter of the Agreement. A cancellation made pursuant to this 
provision shall be effective when Arizona State University receives written notice of the cancellation 
unless the notice specifies a later time. 
7. ARBITRATION: 
Pursuant to AR.S. § 12-1518, disputes under this Agreement shall be resolved through the use of 
arbitration as follows: 
A Cases under the Jurisdictional Limit. In all cases filed in superior court in which the court finds or 
the parties agree that the amount in controversy does not exceed the jurisdictional limit, 
arbitration shall be used, unless all parties file a written stipulation waiving the arbitration 
requirement, and the court waives the arbitration requirement on a showing of good cause. 
8. FUNDING: 
This Agreement is not an obligation of or a commitment of funds, or a basis for a transfer of funds, but 
rather a statement of understanding between the Parties concerning the sharing and use of 
confidential information related to the purposes of this Agreement. Expenditures by each party are 
subject to that party's budgetary processes and to the availability of funds and resources pursuant to 
applicable laws, regulations, and policies of the respective parties. 
9. INSURANCE 
The Navajo County Medical Examiner and Arizona State University are self-insured for liability per 
AR.S.41-621. 
10. BACKGROUND 
The Navajo County Public Health Services District/Navajo County Medical Examiner's Office (ME), by 
collaborating with Arizona State University's Center for Violence Prevention and Community Safety (the 
Center), is proposing to the Centers for Disease Control the establishment of the Arizona Violent Death 
Reporting System (AVDRS). To date, 17 states across the country have established such systems that 
compile statewide, comprehensive data on violent deaths by collecting information from death certificates, 
medical examiner reports, police departments, and other appropriate local agencies. Objectives of these 
statewide surveillance systems include providing comprehensive snapshots of violent incidents to further 
understand such events and inforrning communities, policy makers, planners, and decision makers on 
violent deaths so that preventive programs can be enacted. Establishment of the AVDRS will enable 
increased scientific understanding of violent injury through research, translation of research findings into 
prevention strategies, and the dissemination of knowledge of violent injury and prevention to

<!-- page 166 -->
professionals and the public. Data collection for the project will be sponsored by the Centers for Disease 
Control. 
11. PURPOSE 
This Memorandum of Agreement establishes the basis for the ME to share with the Center vital records 
information on deaths occurring in Arizona. The records will be used for the purpose of contributing to the 
AVDRS by providing accurate, comprehensive, and objective information regarding violence-related 
morbidity and mortality. 
The sharing of vital records data will be in accordance with the terms and conditions stated in this 
agreement and predicated on the mutual assurance that all unique identifiers in these data will be 
protected and kept strictly confidential. 
12. SCOPE OF WORK 
I. ME agrees to provide or continue to provide the Center with the following: 
a. Confidential medical examiner records related to incident narrative, person type (victim/suspect), 
name address, age, sex, race, ethnicity, when and where (injury/death), cause of death ICD 
coders), manner of death, additional person descriptors, alcohol and drug tests, wounds, 
associated circumstances, victim suspect relationship, history of victim abuse, suspect was victim 
caretaker, firearm descriptors, poison details, weapon used by/on person and any other available 
person descriptors for individuals residing within or events occurring within Arizona. 
i. The above noted data will be made available to the Center from January 1, 2008 
and forward. 
ii. The above noted data will be made available to the Center on a monthly basis. 
iii. It is understood that at the present time the Pima County Medical Examiner 
sometimes assists the ME and data from these investigations will be made 
available to the Center. 
b. Within this agreement it is understood that at least 99% of death reports (and associated data) 
are available within 90 days of death and are then ready to be shared with ASU. 
c. The name or names of the ME employees that will provide the information designated under 
Section I. Immediate notification will occur when there are any changes in this list of employees. 
II. The Center agrees to provide or continue to provide the ME with: 
a. A description of the security measures that are in place to maintain the confidentiality of the data 
being received. These measures at a minimum should follow the recommendations for the 
"Security Considerations for Applicants" prepared by the Human Subjects Review Board of the 
ADHS. 
b. Co Analytical support for matters related to data collected as part of the Arizona Violent Death 
Reporting System. The scope, nature, and frequency of the support will be mutually agreed upon 
by both parties in writing after funding has been secured. 
III. Confidential Information: 
a. The parties agree to provide information following a mutually agreeable format and that the 
information shall be marked confidential if disclosed in written or other tangible form, or if 
disclosed orally or visually, identified as confidential at the time of disclosure and reduced to 
writing and marked confidential and transmitted to the receiving party within thirty (30) days of the 
initial disclosure.

<!-- page 167 -->
b. The Center will not use, or disclose to any third party, INFORMATION of ME in any manner 
whatsoever except for the PURPOSE, and will require that its employees and agents who have 
access to such information maintain the same in strict confidence subject to the same for three 
(3) years after the termination of this Agreement; provided that the Center's obligations hereunder 
shall not apply to information that was already known to the receiving party prior to the time of 
first disclosure, as demonstrated by contemporaneous, written documentation; or 
i. At the time of disclosure is in the public domain, or after the date of the 
disclosure, lawfully becomes a part of the public domain other than through 
breach of this Agreement by the receiving party; or 
ii. Is received without any obligation of confidentiality from a third party having a 
legal right to disclose the same; or 
iii. Is independently developed by the receiving party by individuals without access 
to such infonmation, as demonstrated by contemporaneous, written 
documentation; or 
iv. Is required to be disclosed by the receiving party pursuant to a legally 
enforceable order, subpoena, or other regulation ("ORDER"), provided, however, 
that the receiving party promptly notifies the disclosing party in advance of such 
disclosure and discloses only that INFORMATION necessary to comply with said 
ORDER. 
IV. The parties agree to 
a. Carefully restrict use and access of information to those designated by the Center. Vital records 
provided by ME are for the use of the AVDRS only and no copies may be made of such records 
to provide to other individuals or entities for other purposes. 
b. Prohibit identifying information about a person that was supplied under the terms of this 
agreement from being released to anyone not working on AVDRS data collection, or the 
development of the AVDRS. 
c. Require all officers, agents and employees to keep all such shared information strictly 
confidential. To communicate the requirements for this section to all officers, agents and 
employees, to diScipline all persons who may violate the requirements of this section and to notify 
the originating party in writing within forty-eight (48) hours of any violation and corrective actions 
to be taken.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
CONTRACT FOR THE PROVISION OF MENTAL HEALTH SCREENING AND EVALUATION SERVICES 
BETWEEN THE 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT BOARD OF DIRECTORS 
And 
BRIDGEWAY HEALTH SOLUTIONS OF ARIZONA, INC., d/b/a Arizona Complete Health-Complete Care 
Plan 
 
This Intergovernmental Agreement (“Agreement”) is between  Navajo County Public Health Services 
District, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona (“COUNTY”) and Bridgeway Health Solutions of 
Arizona, Inc., d/b/a Arizona Complete Health-Complete Care (AzCH-CCP). 
 
WHEREAS 
A. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 36 -545.04 the costs of court proceedings and cost of services provided 
by a county for screening and evaluation are a charge against the county in which the patient 
resided or was found before hospitalization; and 
B. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 36-545.06, County shall ensure that the services of a screening agency 
and an evaluation agency are provided for purposes of Arizona Revised Statutes Title 36, 
Chapter 5, Article s 4 and  5 (“Title 36” ) and coordinate that care with the local Regional 
Behavioral Health Authority.   
C. COUNTY has the authority to enter into this Agreement pursuant to A.R.S. §§, 11-251, 11-291 
and 11-297(A)(2).   
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration o f the mutual covenants and undertakings herein, AzCH -CCP and 
COUNTY agree as follows:  
Article 1 – TERM  
1.1 The initial Agreement becomes effective October 1, 2024 through June 30, 20 25 unless further 
amended, extended, or terminated pursuant to the provision of this Agreement. 
1.2 Unless otherwise agreed, this Agreement shall automatically renew for a period of one additional 
year every year unless, at least thirty (30) days prior to renewal, either party gives notice of their 
intent to withdraw from this Agreement.   The maximum number of renewals allowed under this 
Agreement is nine, after which the parties shall negotiate a new Agreement. 
1.3 Any change or modification of this Agreement must be in the form of a written amendment to 
this Agreement signed by duly authorized representatives of both parties.  
Article 2 – SCOPE OF SERVICES 
2.1  AzCH-CCP shall provide through contract with a screening and evaluation agency(ies) that serves  
Navajo County, all the pre -petition screening and evaluation services required of the COUNTY 
under Title 36 of the Arizona Revised Statutes. 
2.2 AzCH-CCP or its contractor shall, in collaboration with COUNTY , have discretion to select and 
contract with all behavioral health and other providers needed to perform services under this 
Agreement. If a provider and/or service are located outside of Navajo County, the County will be 
notified 30 days prior to the change occurring.  AzCH-CCP or its contractor shall ensure consistent 
provision of pre -petition screening and evaluation services in the event any  subcontractor is

<!-- page 176 -->
2 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
unable to provide such services due to (1) a conflict of interest, (2) loss of critical staff, or (3) any 
other event preventing them from fulfilling the statutory obligations of Title 36. 
2.3 With regard to detainees in the Navajo County Jail, screening and evaluation services shall be 
provided.   Any transportation required for inmates will be provided by Navajo County through 
the Navajo County jail detention staff.  
2.4 To the extent obligated by law, COUNTY shall retain financial responsibility  for Court proceeding 
expenses for commitment action brought under Title 36, including the costs of independent 
evaluators and the fees and costs for the attorneys for the prosecution, and the court -appointed 
defense attorneys.  
As per the AHCCCS Contractor Operations Manual Section 437, the cost of  Court Ordered  
Evaluation (COE) services provided as part of a legal proceeding under A.R.S. § 36 -520 et. seq. is 
the financial responsibility of the County in which the individual resided or was found (i.e., county 
of origin).    Under A.R.S. § 36 -546.06, the cost of Pre -Petition Screening and COE is a county 
responsibility.  AzCH-CCP should not use Title XIX/XXI funding for COE. 
For any individual who has been admitted to an Evaluation Agency for COE, county responsibility 
for payment shall end when the evaluation period ends.   The evaluation is deemed to end when 
one of the following occur: 
a.) A Petition for Court Ordered Treatment (COT) is filed with the Court 
b.) The individual agrees to voluntary treatment, or 
c.) The individual is released from COE. 
Meeting any of these conditions does not always indicate that the person is no longer physically 
at the location at which the evaluation occurred.   When county payment responsibility for 
screening and evaluation ends, financial responsibility shifts to either the individual’s private 
insurance or, the titled individual or, if an enrolled person, to the Title XIX/XXI Contractor who 
shall pay for all medically necessary services, thereafter, including services associated with the 
period of time between the filing of the Petition for COT and the hearing set for the purposes of a 
judicial determination for the need for COT. 
2.5  In an ongoing mutual effort to address identified needs and issues within the publicly funded 
behavioral health system, the parties agree to facilitate meetings to discuss and work to resolve 
those needs and issues.  In addition, AzCH- CCP reports to C OUNTY shall consist of Monthly 
Narrative Summary; Historical Cost and Service Trends; Detailed Monthly Client Activity Logs as it 
relates to COT , Detailed Year to Date Activity Logs; Monthly Claims: Redacted and Un-redacted 
versions (including date of service, name, service description, procedure code, unit price, service 
count, charge amount, and total charge).  
The parties agree to work collaboratively to determine the appropriate level of additional 
reporting so that Navajo County can verify the appropriate level of contractor performance and 
efficiency, while not placing undue administrative burden on AzCH -CCP, or its contractors. Any 
proposed solutions shall be reasonable in the circumstances and result in greater transparency, 
accessibility, availability, coordination,  and delivery of Title 36 behavioral health services to 
COUNTY residents.

<!-- page 177 -->
3 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
2.6 AzCH-CCP shall require any providers performing services under this Agreement to undergo 
regular training in Title 36 law and processes. AzCH- CCP or the service providers may seek such 
training from the Navajo County Attorney’s Office or such other provider of their choosing. 
2.7 Under the terms of this Agreement neither AzCH-CCP nor its contractors are obligated to provide 
or pay for:  
 A. Any behavioral health services except for the screening and evaluation services associated with 
A.R.S. Title 36, Chapter 5, Articles 4 and 5.  
 B. Any screening or evaluation services when no behavioral health agency licensed to provide 
such services in Navajo County is willing or available to contract for such services  or unwilling or 
unable to perform under such contract after the parties’ good -faith efforts to facilitate such 
performance. This section shall be interpreted as a condition subsequent to this agreement.   
Should a case arise in which there is no screening or evaluation agency in Navajo County is willing 
to provide services, Navajo County and AzCH-CCP shall collaborate on finding an appropriate and 
licensed agency outside of the Navajo County to perform such services through telemedicine and 
tele court.    
2.8 AzCH-CCP shall ensure that its contractor shall reasonably seek informed consent for treatment 
from the patients, on a voluntary basis, in the pre-petition screening, petition for evaluation and 
petition for treatment phases of Title 36. 
2.9 AzCH-CCP shall ensure that its contractor implements a plan to ensure that patients referred for 
Title 36 evaluations and who may qualify as Seriously Mentally Ill (SMI) pursuant to Title 19, also 
receive SMI evaluations. 
 
Article 3 – FUNDING 
3.1 Payment for services shall be based upon the cost of services provided, billed at the rates specified 
in Schedule A.  AzCH-CCP or AzCH-CCP’s contractor shall screen each patient for Title XIX eligibility 
status and for other third-party payer coverage. The County is payer of last resort with respect to 
the Title 36 screening and evaluation services d escribed in the Agreemen t except for Title XIX 
covered services provided to Title XIX eligible persons. With respect to those services, AHCCCS is 
the payor of last resort, and the County is the payor of last resort with respect to all other sources 
of third-party liability other than AHCCCS.  
3.2 AzCH-CCP shall submit a monthly expenditure report to the COUNTY.  The AzCH-CCP expenditure 
report shall include  the actual services provided , the total monthly third-party liability receipts 
collected, and the cumulative total of third-party liability receipts collected for the fiscal year. The 
format of which shall be agreed upon by the COUNTY and AzCH-CCP.  
3.3 The expenditure report shall include the 8% administrative fee for AzCH-CCP which is part of the 
quarterly payment to AzCH-CCP.

<!-- page 178 -->
4 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
3.4 By the first day of each quarter, COUNTY agrees to pay AzCH-CCP $105,000.001 which will be 
applied toward the amount reported under Section 3.2.    
  
 Upon receipt of quarterly allocation, AzCH-CCP will pass thru the funds to its Contractor. 
 
AzCH-CCP shall submit a quarterly statement reconciling the funds paid by the COUNTY, to include 
the collected third party liability, against amounts expended by AzCH-CCP within 45 days following 
the end of quarters 1, 2, & 3. (No adjustments to funding shall be made following the end of 
quarters 1, 2, & 3).  
 
On or before April 1st,  AzCH-CCP shall submit to COUNTY a request for additional reimbursement 
(if any is warranted), based on projected expenditures and third-party liability collections through 
the end of the fiscal year.  
 
On or before March 15
th, AzCH-CCP shall also submit a projection of anticipated resources needed 
for the next fiscal year so that the COUNTY may use the projection for budget preparation. 
 
AzCH-CCP shall submit an annual reconciliation of the funds to the COUNTY 45 days after the end 
of quarter 4.  Any amount remaining unspent by the end of this Agreement shall be returned to 
the COUNTY within 60 days after the end of quarter . Any expenditures reported for services 
provided in excess of the COUNTY payments shall reimbursed to AzCH-CCP with 60 days after the 
end of quarter 4. 
 
3.5 AzCH-CCP agrees that it will not seek any compensation from COUNTY under this agreement other 
than compensation set forth in this Article.  
 
Article 4 – INSURANCE 
4.1 The parties acknowledge that the COUNTY is self-insured, in whole or in part,  pursuant to 
statutory authority. The parties agree that the general liability coverage and the professional 
liability coverage afforded by these insurance/self-insurance programs are sufficient to meet the 
purposes of this Agreement.  
4.2 AzCH-CCP shall maintain during the entire term of this Agreement commercial general liability, 
automobile liability, workers compensation and professional liability insurance coverage in 
amounts satisfactory to the County.  AzCH-CCP shall name the COUNTY as an additional insured 
with respect to liability arising out of the activities performed by or on behalf of AzCH-CCP.  
Article 5 – INDEMNIFICATION  
5.1  Each party (as “indemnitor”) agrees to indemnify, defend and hold harmless the other party (as 
“indemnitee”) from and against any and all claims, losses, liability, costs, or expenses (including 
reasonable attorney’s fees) (hereinafter collectively referred to as “claims”) arising out of bodily 
injury of any person (including death) or property damage, but only to the extent that such claims

Article 6 – TERMINATION  
6.1  This Agreement may be terminated as may be provided by law , including A.R.S. 38 -511 in the 
event of a conflict of interest.

Article 10 – MISCELLANEOUS 
10.1 The parties agree that all of the conditions set forth herein are material to the Agreement and a 
breach of any condition is a breach of the Agreement.  
10.2 Each Article of this Agreement stands alone. If any provision of this Agreement is held to be invalid 
or unenforceable, the remaining provisions shall continue to be valid  and enforceable to the full 
extent permitted by law.  
10.3 The failure of either party t o insist in any one or more instances upon the full and complete  
performance of any of the terms or provisions of this Agreement to be performed on the part of 
the other or to take any action permitted as a result thereof shall not be construed as a waiver or 
relinquishment of the right to insist upon full and complete performance of the same or any other 
covenant or condition either in the past or in the future. The acceptance by either party of sums 
less than may be due and owing at any time shall not be construed as an accord and satisfaction.  
10.4  Captions and headings are for index purposes only and shall not be used in construing this 
Agreement.  
10.5 This Agreement shall be interpreted in accordance with Arizona law.  
10.6 The parties agree to use arbitration to the extent required by A.R.S. § 12-1518. Each party shall 
bear its own costs of arbitration and shall equally share in the cost of the arbitrator.  
10.7 This Agreement is subject to the provisions of A.R.S. §38-511. 
10.8 The parties agree to retain all records related to this Agreement for five (5) years after completion 
of the Agreement and make such records available to the other party at reasonable times 
determined by the parties.

This is an annual contract with the option to renew up to four one-year terms to provide Inmate Food
Services (three meals per day, seven days per week, and three hundred sixty-five days per year and three
hundred sixty-six days during leap years) for our County Jail, both Adult and Juvenile Inmates. Sealed
proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors office and publicly opened on July 17, 2024. Two
proposals were received. The proposals were evaluated per the criteria set forth in the Request for
Proposal.
The Evaluation Committee is recommending awarding of Contract B24-05-007 Inmate Food Services to
Trinity Services Group, Inc.

Background: 
IIMC grants the CMC designation only to those clerks who complete demanding education requirements
and who have a record of significant contributions to their local government, their community and state. 
Please help me congratulate Leah on becoming a Certified Municipal Clerk through ASU and the
International Institute of Municipal Clerks. Her hard work and perseverance has paid off. In order to earn
the CMC designation, Leah attended extensive educational programs which included 60 points of
educational hours and 50 points in the experience category. Each educational program that Leah attended
was followed up by a learning assessment and report on what she learned and how she would put that
information into practice in her work area. A part of Leah’s experience is that she currently serves as the
Vice President of the Arizona County Clerks Association.

The Executive &

We are an Association of Arizona’s 15 county
governments.
We do non-partisan research and policy work to
support decision making at the county, state, and
federal level.
Counties created CSA because it is more effective
to centralize this work.
Each county benefits from what we do and
benefits from collaborating with each other.
The result of this is better government and better
public service.

contribute to, or take away from, the recruitment, job

detention personnel in Arizona.

Study by faculty at ASU & UA
Funded through $300,000 Regents’ Community Grant from ABOR
Study by faculty at ASU, NAU & UA
Funded through $800,000 Regents’ Community Grant from ABOR
 Select Findings for Counties
1.  Sheriffs’ offices had unique challenges & experiences.
2.  Lateral movements across agencies is rare.
3.  Younger employees or recruits were less satisfied with work-life
balance or looking for more regular hours or part time options.
4.  Changes to salary, housing stipends, appear to have strongest impact
on recruitment and retention likelihood.
5.  Hiring environment has changed, requiring new strategies to recruit
into public safety.

Elections
• Resolution of the “auto recount” issue.
• Elimination, or restriction, of a county’s
ability to establish vote centers.
• Limitations on precinct size.
• “Privacy flaps” for early voting ballots.
• Restriction of early voting eligibility.
• Mandatory on-site tabulation.
• Shift of AVID funding responsibility to state.
• Permitting Board and councilmembers to
engage on “call to the public” topics.
• Required “call to the public”  for volunteer
boards and commissions.
• Requires a projected date of completion for
PRRs within 5 days.
• Submission of settlement agreements to the
Legislature.
• Legislative vacancy appointments.
• Tax lien sales.
• County split.
• Property tax refund for expenses incurred
to mitigate certain nuisance.
• Procurement practices (reauthorization;
certifications required prior to).
• Electric vehicle registration fee increase.
• Type 03 school district fix.
• Creation of theme park districts.
• Requires a supermajority vote to raise
revenues.
• Requires supermajority approval at the
ballot to pass bonds.
• Title 36 processes.
• Secure behavioral health facilities.
• Jury trials for parental right termination.
• Prohibitions on psychiatric evaluations and
background checks for elected officials.
• Reopening of CORP.
• Traumatic event counseling for constables.

disease.
• Expansion of fireworks permissions.

businessowners.
• Preemptions relating to wastewater
permitting.

• Expansion of “cottage food,” or the “tamale
bill.”
• By-right zoning for religious institutions.
• Shot clocks for “applications for
development.”
• Exemptions from inspection, building
permits, and zoning for tiny homes, ADUs,
detached garages, and greenhouses.
• Requiring several in-person services for
zoning violations.

Subject:
Presentation of the Navajo County Sheriff's Office Coordinated Re-Entry Services program
Background: 
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office is in the implementation stages of a program designed to assist
incarcerated individuals who are suffering from mental health, substance abuse, medical, and other social
disorders and/or issues as they re-enter society after their release. While still in development, the Sheriff's
Office would like to share the progress and vision of the program and the many partnerships involved.

Introduction
In recent years, the increase in opioids in the
drug supply, particularly fentanyl has only
exacerbated and expanded the devastation to
families and communities throughout Navajo
County.
A recent report from the CDC in 2022
indicates over 107,000 deaths nation wide and
a steady increase every  year since.

<!-- page 251 -->
Introduction
The Sheriff’s Office is committed to
providing critical care and services to those
who have been most affected by the opioid
epidemic by creating program services for
those who are placed into custody.

<!-- page 253 -->
Why is that Important
The majority of those affected have contact
within the model at some point with the
greatest amount being post arrest.
According to the Bureau of Justice, more than
60% of the jail population have a (SUD)
Substance Use Disorder as compared to 5% in
a non-carceral setting.

<!-- page 254 -->
Why is that Important
In 2022 there were 74 overdoses reported, with 12 deaths. 
These numbers only reflect cases reported by state mandated
reporting requirements by law enforcement agencies and/or
EMS.  97% of these cases received Naloxone.
In 2023 there were 97 overdoses reported, with 6 deaths. 
These numbers only reflect cases reported by state mandated
reporting requirements by law enforcement agencies and/or
EMS.  Percentage of 2023 cases receiving Naloxone is not
yet available.

<!-- page 255 -->
Initiative
Creating a comprehensive program that
begins at the front door (reception intake) and
continues through incarceration to back door
(release).
We can affect the greatest amount of change
to serve the community and public safety.

<!-- page 257 -->
Services
Stage 1
Conduct a screening on every individual entering the detention facility during
the booking process.
The screening is a series of questions related to:
Mental Health
Substance Abuse
Socioeconomic Needs

<!-- page 258 -->
Services
Stage 2
Coordinated Treatment Options for Individual Needs

<!-- page 259 -->
Services
Stage 3
 Facilitate Services for each individual needing treatment.
In-custody Treatment Availability
Develop meaningful connections for warm hand off.
Barriers to serve this population are constantly fluctuating. Often
individuals are released from jail prior to having service connections.
This program identifies this need and provides those opportunities to
connect them to services prior to release.

<!-- page 260 -->
Services
Stage 4
 Re-integration.
Return individuals into society with appropriate treatment 
services and family support integration.
By providing coordinated care for behavioral service, peer support,
housing, transportation and other social stability needs the opportunity
for a successful civil, prosocial society member is increased. Thus,
reducing recidivisms.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a.        
Meeting Date: 08/27/2024  
Title: Pinedale Elementary School Restoration, Contract B24-02-008
Submitted For: Grant Evans, Capital Projects Manager 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Administrative Services
Presented By: Grant Evans
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract No. B24-02-008 for the Pinedale Elementary School Restoration
Project to American Commercial Contracting and to authorize change order authority to the County
Manager for 10% of the contract amount, and authorize the County Manager to execute the necessary
documents.
Background:
This contract will replace roofing and repair structural elements at Pinedale Elementary School located at
1264 Pinedale Rd, Pinedale, AZ 85934.  The base bid includes the replacement of the roof and repairing
structural elements of the roof.  Additive Alternate One includes the removal of the main entry porch roof
structure (east porch) and north porch roof structure, and all supporting wood posts.  Additive Alternate
Two includes the removal of existing concrete flatwork, installing new concrete flatwork and completing
grading and drainage improvements.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Government Center,
Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T., July 24, 2024.  No bids were accepted after 3:00 p.m. M.S.T.. 
The bid was publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m.  One bid was received and American
Commercial Contracting is the responsible, responsive bidder.  
Recommendation: 
Staff is recommending award of Contract No. B24-02-008 Pinedale Elementary School Restoration to
American Commercial Contracting for the Base Bid, plus Additive Alternate One and Additive Alternate
Two.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 25
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 179,841.09
Fiscal Impact:
The total amount requested is $179,841.09.  $163,491.90 for the construction contract (base bid plus

<!-- page 268 -->
The total amount requested is $179,841.09.  $163,491.90 for the construction contract (base bid plus
additive alternates one and two) and an additional $16,349.19 is requested as a contingency fund for
project changes.
The County is the recipient of a Historic Preservation Grant from the Arizona State Parks Board for this
project.  The Grant is providing $100,790 in funding for the project and the County has a required grant
match in the amount of $67,194.  This match contribution was previously approved through Resolution
#35-22.  After grant and match funds in the amount of $15,271 were expended on the project design, there
are $152,713 in remaining grant funds for the construction.  The $27,128.09 of remaining funds needed for
the construction contract and the contingency funds are budgeted from General Funds.

CONTRACT 
 
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of August 27, 2024 (the “Effective Date”) by 
and between Navajo County (“County”) and American Commercial Contracting (“Contractor”). 
 
1. SCOPE OF WORK:  Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and labor 
required for performing all work for PINEDALE ELEMENTARY SCHOOL RESTORATION 
PROJECT, CONTRACT, #B24-02-008 for the Base Bid, plus Additive Alternate One 
and Additive Alternate Two, in accordance with the Contract Documents, in a workmanlike 
manner to the satisfaction of County through its County Engineer. 
 
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS:  The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and the 
attached  Notice of Invitation for Bids,  General Provisions,  Arizona State Parks-
Approved Project Scope and Special Conditions,  Arizona State Parks-General 
Conditions Applicable to Project Grants,  Proposal dated July 24, 2024,  Bid Schedule, 
 Bid Bond,  Affidavit of Non-Collusion,  Subcontractor Certification,  
Qualifications/Projects/References,  Performance Bond,  Labor and Materials Bond,  
Contractor Performance Warranty,  Certificate of Insurance,  Plans, and addenda 
thereto, if any.  If there is any conflict in the documents, this Contract shall control over all 
others; the Arizona State Parks-Approved Project Scope and Special Conditions and the 
Arizona State Parks-General Conditions Applicable to Project Grants shall control over the 
General Provisions. 
 
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION:  Contractor shall furnish all materials, equipment 
and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear of all claims, liens and 
charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in the Proposal. 
 
4. CANCELLATION:  This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-511 
(concerning conflicts of interest). 
 
5. PAYMENTS:   County shall pay to Contractor a not-to-exceed sum of one hundred sixty-
three thousand four hundred ninety-one dollars and ninety cents ($163,491.90) as payment 
in full for all services rendered by Contractor pursuant to this Agreement.  Payment shall be 
made for roofing material once it is delivered to the jobsite.  The balance will be retained by 
the Owner until the completion and acceptance of all work.  Invoices shall be paid no later 
than 14 days after each invoice has been verified and approved by the Project Manager. 
 
6. CERTIFICATIONS:  
 
A. A. PROHIBITION OF BOYCOTT OF ISRAEL:  Contractor certifies that neither it nor 
its subcontractors are currently or will be engaged in, for the duration of this Contract 
engage in, a boycott of Israel as defined by A.R.S. § 35-393 et seq.  Violation of this 
certification may result in an action by the County up to and including termination of 
the Contract. 
 
B. WRITTEN CERTIFICATION PURSUANT TO A.R.S. §35-394:  Pursuant to A.R.S. § 
35-394, Contractor certifies that it is not currently using, and agrees for the duration of 
this Agreement to not use (1) the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s 
Republic of China; (2) any goods or services produced by the forced labor of ethnic

<!-- page 272 -->
Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of China; and (3) any contractors, subcontractors, 
or suppliers that use the forced labor of ethnic Uyghurs in the People’s Republic of 
China.  The Subrecipient further acknowledges and agrees that (1) if the Subrecipient 
becomes aware during the term of this Agreement that it is not in compliance with this 
certification that the Subrecipient will notify the County within five (5) business days 
after becoming aware of the noncompliance; and (2) if the Subrecipient does not 
provide the County with a written certification that the Subrecipient has remedied the 
noncompliance within one hundred eighty (180) after giving notice thereof, the same 
shall constitute a material breach of this Agreement, which shall then terminate 
automatically, except that if the Agreement termination date occurs before the end of 
the remedy period, this Agreement terminates on the Agreement’s termination date.  
The County retains the legal right to inspect the records of the Contractor and all 
Subcontractors to ensure compliance with this certification for the duration of this

BASE BID:  The base bid for the project is to replace the roofing and repair the structural elements of the roof.  This work 
includes replacing sections of the rafter tails where rotted, replacing rotted sheathing at roof overhangs, and any other roof 
sheathing found to be in poor condition, all as described on the drawings.
Proposals received from:
ADDITIVE ALTERNATES
The following additive alternates may be added to the above total base bid price amount, in any combination, by the County.  
If added, the work shall be completed by the Contractor.  Additive Alternate One will be taken before Additive Alternate Two.
Additive Alternate One for the project is to complete the removal of the two porch roof structures and their supporting wood 
posts.  These two porches are located on the north and east sides of the building.  Additive Alternate One also includes 
stucco repair and painting at the newly exposed faces of the building and installation of new rain gutters and downspouts at 
the entrances as shown on the drawings.
Additive Alternate Two for the project is to complete the removal of the existing concrete flatwork as identified on the 
drawings.  This includes the associated steps, ramps, and handrails.  Additive Alternate Two also includes the site grading 
and drainage improvements; including the earthwork, existing culvert removals and installation of new culverts.  This additive 
alternate also includes the construction of the new concrete sidewalks, steps, ramps and handrails, all as identified on the 
drawings.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. b.        
Meeting Date: 08/27/2024  
Title: Clay Springs Community Center Roof Replacement
Submitted For: Grant Evans, Capital Projects Manager 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Administrative Services
Presented By: Grant Evans
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract for the Clay Springs Community Center Roof Replacement to
Mountain Roof Repairs, LLC and to authorize change order authority to the County Manager for 10% of
the contract amount, and authorize the County Manager to execute the necessary documents.
Background:
This contract is for the replacement of the roof at the Clay Springs Community Center located at 2114
Sandstone Road, Clay Springs, AZ 85923.  The existing asphalt shingle roof will be replaced with a new
standing seam metal roof system.
Through adoption of Resolution Number 01-2023 on January 24, 2023, the Board of Supervisors selected
this project as one of three projects to undertake with the county's Community Development Block Grant
(CDBG) funds.  This grant funding comes through the Arizona Department of Housing (ADOH).  The
Northern Arizona Council of Governments (NACOG) oversees the planning of annual allocations and
provides technical assistance for these projects.  The county has worked closely with NACOG throughout
the bid process to ensure our compliance with all terms and conditions of the CDBG program.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Government Center,
Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. on Wednesday, July 31, 2024.  No bids were accepted after 3:00 p.m.
M.S.T..  The bid was publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m.  Three bids were received and
Mountain Roof Repairs, LLC is the lowest responsible, responsive bidder.  
Recommendation: 
Staff is recommending award of contract for the replacement of the roof with a new metal roof system at
the Clay Springs Community Center to Mountain Roof Repairs, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 25
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $159,000
Fiscal Impact:
There is $159,000 worth of CDBG grant funds for the construction of this project.  The contract with

<!-- page 275 -->
There is $159,000 worth of CDBG grant funds for the construction of this project.  The contract with
Mountain Roof Repairs, LLC is in the amount of $142,866; therefore, there is no fiscal impact to the
county. The project is fully funded through the CDBG grant and there is no match requirement for this
grant.  The remaining $16,134 of available grant funds are reserved as a contingency fund for project
changes.

during the County’s then-current fiscal year. The County’s obligations under this Contract are 
current expenses subject to the “budget law” and the unfettered legislative discretion of the 
County concerning budgeted purposes and appropriation of funds. Should the County elect not to 
appropriate and budget funds to pay its Contract obligations, this Contract shall be deemed 
terminated at the end of the then-current fiscal year term for which such funds were appropriated 
and budgeted for such purpose, and the County shall be relieved of any subsequent obligation 
under this Contract. The parties agree that the County has no obligation or duty of good faith to 
budget or appropriate the payment of the County’s obligations set forth in this Contract in any 
budget in any fiscal year other than the fiscal year in which this Contract is executed and 
delivered. The County shall be the sole judge and authority in determining the availability of funds 
for its obligations under this Contract. The County shall keep the Contractor informed as to the 
availability of funds for this Contract. The obligation of the County to make any payment pursuant 
to this Contract is not a general obligation or indebtedness of the County. The Contractor hereby 
waives any and all rights to bring any claim against the County from or relating in any way to the 
County’s termination of this Contract pursuant to this paragraph.

STAFF REPORT
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Hearing Date: August 27 th, 2024
Case No: MDP-A 24-001
Action: Master Development Plan Amendment
Applicant/Owner: Steve Kohner/TST Holdings
Project Name: STARLIGHT RIDGE ESTATES III
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Master Development Plan Amendment, subject
to the conditions of this Staff Report. The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission voted to
recommend approval of this Master Development Plan Amendment, subject to the conditions of this
Staff Report.
Location: The property is located in the Starlight Ridge Estates subdivision, near the Wagon Wheel
area, on APNs # 212-05-007N, 212-05-007P, 212-05-007Q, 212-05-007R, 212-05-007S
Size: Five (5) parcels totaling approximately 53 acres.
Supervisor District: V.
Zoning: SD.
Property Owners: TST Holdings
Applicant: TST Holdings
Representative: Steve Kohner.
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:
Consideration of a request by Steve Kohner for a Master Development Plan Amendment from an
approved, approximately 80-unit, single family residential subdivision to be amended to allow for 49
single family lots, 74 townhome units, and changes to the roadways as well as open spaces in the
Starlight Ridge Estates Special Development within the Wagon Wheel area. APN#: 212-05-007N, 212-
05-007P, 212-05-007Q, 212-05-007R, 212-05-007S, T09N, R22E, S08 of the GSRM. Owner: TST
Holdings. Agent: Steve Kohner.

<!-- page 284 -->
Board of Supervisors           P a g e2 | 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:
The land in the vicinity of this project is zoned Special Development or A-General. The Starlight Ridge
Estates #2 subdivision lies directly north of this project. Starlight Ridge Estates Townhouses #2 lies
directly to the east of this project, as does Starlight Ridge Estates #1, and vacant, A-General land sits to
the southeast of this project. The Fort Apache Indian Reservation borders this project to the west, as
well as the south.
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:
There is an existing, approved Master Development Plan for this project. This MDP was amended in
2006, and a tentative plat was approved by the Planning and Zoning Commission in same year. This
approval allowed for 53 acres of the subject parcels to be developed out to a maximum of 80 single-
family home units.
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:
This project is designed to contain the following components, split into two phases of development:
 Phase 1 will consist of 49 single-family lots and will be located on the northern 31.90 acres of
this project.
 Phase 2 will consist of 74 townhome units and will be located on the remaining southern 21.10
acres of this project.
 Connection to the City of Show Low water system.
 Connection to the Pinetop Sanitary Sewer District.
 Fire hydrants installed at 400ft intervals.
 Paved onsite and access roads.
ZONING DISTRICT:
ARTICLE  17 – (SD) – SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONE
Section 1701 – Purpose
1. The purpose of the Special Development zone is to provide for the development of subdivisions and other
land use projects containing various dwelling types, necessary commercial centers, off street parking areas,
open space areas for recreation, and to preserve a rural atmosphere.
2. Deviations from design standards for the specific zone or zones may be permitted provided open spaces are
developed and maintained for the use of the residents and provided further that the parcel size, soil conditions,
terrain, ground cover and other significant natural features favor this type of development.
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:
Comprehensive Plan
Land Use Element

<!-- page 285 -->
Board of Supervisors           P a g e3 | 
Goal 4:  Provide equal opportunity for safe, decent, sanitary, and affordable housing for all
residents of the County and provide a range of residential uses that offer housing opportunities
for all County residents.
Policy 4-B: Promote greater flexibility and economy of land use, provide opportunities in the
Zoning Ordinance for alternative housing types and patterns, planned developments, mixed uses,
and other innovations that reduce development costs.
 The proposed amendments expand the scope of the Starlight Ridge Estates III
development to include a higher number of lots/spaces, as well as add additional mixed
uses to the project – effectively providing for more housing type options for the
community.
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development.
Policy 6-A: Encourage the use of, and provide incentives such as density bonuses for,
implementation of community master plans.
 The proposed amendments to the Master Development Plan for the Starlight Ridge
Estates III development encourage the project to continue.
Goal 7:  Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and technology
uses, where appropriate, to increase employment opportunities.
Policy 7-A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development so as to attract and
locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or other appropriately surfaced access
and are easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities and cities, and in
communities with air and/or rail access.
 The proposed amendments retain the previously approved commercial district and
expand the allowed uses in the commercial district to include a larger range of
commercial ventures.
Goal 8: Reduce the danger from fire for all residents living in a wildland urban interface or near
a National Forest boundary.
Policy 8-C: Discourage high-density development in areas where fire protection and other
emergency services are not readily available.
 The development is within a distance from emergency services that Staff has determined
to be reasonable for the proposed density of the development.
Policy 8-D: Continue to require multiple access routes into new large subdivisions and other
major developments for emergency purposes and to improve traffic circulation pursuant to the
Subdivision Regulations & Requirements.
 The Site Plan indicates two primary access points along Wagon Wheel Lane to the west,
with secondary access available on Rim Road from the north and south.
Policy 8-E: Continue the review by the Fire Districts in the approval of subdivisions and
development projects within their respective jurisdictions.
 The Fire District under which their jurisdiction falls has reviewed the proposal and has

<!-- page 286 -->
Board of Supervisors           P a g e4 | 
provided feedback to the applicant and Staff, as well as a Will Serve letter.
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner.
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the County
where services, including water and sewer systems, paved rights-of-way, and schools, are
available or can be created with new development.
 This project has indicated that there are existing nearby water, sewer, and electricity
utilities in the vicinity of this project, and the applicant has indicated their intent to
connect to these services. In addition, there are existing paved rights-of-way, and the
applicant has indicated their intent to pave the internal roads of this project.
Policy 9-D: Encourage in-fill opportunities in the County to capitalize on existing infrastructure
and reduce growth-related costs.
 The proposed amendments to the Starlight Ridge Estates III development serve to uphold
the previous intention to complete the development, and further uphold the opportunity
to utilize the pre-existing infrastructure.
Circulation Element
Goal 1: Provide a range of circulation options that are safe and efficient and that complement
local communities and the natural environment.
Policy 1-B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive and
efficient development and growth patterns that support adjacent land uses, complement the
character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate impacts on the natural
environment.
 The proposed access points utilize existing roadways. Additionally, the proposed
circulatory roadways within the development are complimentary to the surrounding
neighborhood roadways.
Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property.
Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and legal
access for all properties.
 All proposed access points to the development have been determined to be safe and legal.
Policy 4-E: Include consideration of, and planning for, pedestrian, equestrian, handicapped-
accessibility and bicycle use when designing circulation infrastructure improvements through
addition of trails, sidewalks, signage, pavement markings, or other treatments.
 The proposed amendments include a walking trail along the western border of this
project.
Analysis:
The proposed Master Development Plan Amendment meets all relevant goals and policies of the Navajo
County Zoning Ordinance and the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan as described above.

Planning and Development Services Staff have received thirteen (13) comments in opposition of this
project as of August 7th, 2024.
FINDINGS OF FACT:
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes
and Article 29.
This item was posted in the Wednesday, July 31st, 2024 edition of the Holbrook Tribune.  Postcards
were mailed to neighbors within 300 feet of the property. The property was posted with public
hearing notices.
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan.
The proposed amendments meet several applicable goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive
Plan, as noted within this report.
3. The proposed amendments are compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in
which the property is located.
The proposed amendments are compatible with permissible uses within the Special Development
zoning district, as proposed.
4. The proposed amendments are compatible with the current and likely future uses of
properties in the vicinity of the development.
The proposed amendments have been determined to be compatible with current and likely future
uses per the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.
5. The proposed amendments will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and
general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the development.
Given the nature of the property and the zoning of the neighborhood, there should be minimal
adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the
development as it has been proposed to be amended.

PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:
The Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff, as well as the Navajo County Planning and
Zoning Commission, recommend approval of the Master Development Plan Amendment, subject to the
following conditions:
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County permits required for each lot must be obtained and adhered to.
2. This Master Development Site Plan shall allow for a maximum of 49 single family lots, and 74
townhome units.
3. A Traffic Impact Analysis (TIA), Master Drainage Plan, Floodplain Study, Sanitary Sewer Collection
System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design Report for the development, prepared by a
Professional Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall be submitted to and approved by the
County Engineer’s office before the Tentative Plat and/or Site Plan Review is submitted to the
Planning and Zoning Commission.
4. All required offsite improvements identified in the TIA, Master Drainage Plan, Floodplain Study,
Sanitary Sewer Collection System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design Report shall be
constructed or financially assured before the stage of development is constructed that requires the
improvement.
5. Sufficient off-road drainage easements shall be made available to accommodate major drainage as
required.
6. Drainage structures, including detention or retention ponds, shall be proposed with the Final Plat and/or
Site Plan Review, as required.
7. The southern access to Rim Road and Wagon Wheel Road shall be required, and a continuous paved
roadway segment shall be provided.
8. A minimum of 280 feet of unobstructed sight visibility shall be provided at the access points along
Wagon Wheel Road.
9. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations shall be met before approval of the Tentative
Plat and/or Site Plan Review. Variances shall be requested in accordance with Section 4.12.
10. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and Requirements, Flood Control
Ordinance, Building Safety Ordinance, and applicable codes shall be adhered to and approved at the
proper Tentative Plat, Final, or Building Permit and construction phase (to include, without limitation,
the Traffic Impact Analysis, Drainage Study, Floodplain Study, Construction Improvement Plans, and
Grading Plans).
11. Approval of the Master Development Site Plan is conditioned upon the submittal for a request for either
a Tentative Plat or for a Site Plan Review to the Planning and Zoning Commission, along with proof
of compliance with the above conditions, within 24 months of the date of Board of Supervisors

<!-- page 289 -->
Board of Supervisors           P a g e7 | 
Resolution. If this condition is not met, approval shall be revoked, and the Master Development Site
Plan shall become null and void. This condition shall be conclusively deemed to have been satisfied
unless the Board of Supervisors causes a notice of revocation to be recorded in the Office of the Navajo
County Recorder within 30 days after the expiration of the 24-month period (or any extension thereof).

RESOLUTION ____-24
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING
A MASTER DEVELOPMENT PLAN AMENDMENT FOR STARLIGHT RIDGE
ESTATES III SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT
WHEREAS, an application for a Master Development Plan Amendment was duly filed
on January 18th, 2024, by Steve Kohner to allow for 49 single family lots, 74 townhome units, and
changes to the roadways as well as open spaces in the Starlight Ridge Estates Special Development
within the Wagon Wheel area; and
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN # 212-05-007N, 212-05-007P, 212-05-007Q, 212-05-007R, 212-05-007S, Township 09
North, Range 22 East, Section 08 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian; and
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on July 18th, 2024; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Master Development Plan Amendment is consistent with the public health, safety and general
welfare and should be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  the Board of Supervisors hereby
approves the Master Development Plan Amendment, subject to the following conditions:
1. All Federal, State, and Navajo County permits required for each lot must be obtained and
adhered to.
2. This Master Development Site Plan shall allow for a maximum of 49 single family lots,
and 74 townhome units.
3. A Traffic Impact Analysis (TIA), Master Drainage Plan, Floodplain Study, Sanitary Sewer
Collection System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design Report for the
development, prepared by a Professional Engineer, licensed in the State of Arizona, shall
be submitted to and approved by the County Engineer’s office before the Tentative Plat
and/or Site Plan Review is submitted to the Planning and Zoning Commission.
4. All required offsite improvements identified in the TIA, Master Drainage Plan, Floodplain
Study, Sanitary Sewer Collection System Design Report, and Water Distribution Design

<!-- page 291 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Report shall be constructed or financially assured before the stage of development is
constructed that requires the improvement.
5. Sufficient off-road drainage easements shall be made available to accommodate major
drainage as required.
6. Drainage structures, including detention or retention ponds, shall be proposed with the
Final Plat and/or Site Plan Review, as required.
7. The southern access to Rim Road and Wagon Wheel Road shall be required, and a
continuous paved roadway segment shall be provided.
8. A minimum of 280 feet of unobstructed sight visibility shall be provided at the access
points along Wagon Wheel Road.
9. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations shall be met before approval of
the Tentative Plat and/or Site Plan Review. Variances shall be requested in accordance with
Section 4.12.
10. All technical requirements of the Subdivision Regulations and Requirements, Flood
Control Ordinance, Building Safety Ordinance, and applicable codes shall be adhered to
and approved at the proper Tentative Plat, Final, or Building Permit and construction phase
(to include, without limitation, the Traffic Impact Analysis, Drainage Study, Floodplain
Study, Construction Improvement Plans, and Grading Plans).
11. Approval of the Master Development Site Plan is conditioned upon the submittal for a
request for either a Tentative Plat or for a Site Plan Review to the Planning and Zoning
Commission, along with proof of compliance with the above conditions, within 24 months
of the date of Board of Supervisors Resolution. If this condition is not met, approval shall

RESOLUTION ____-24
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING
A MASTER DEVELOPMENT PLAN AMENDMENT FOR STARLIGHT RIDGE
ESTATES III SPECIAL DEVELOPMENT
WHEREAS, an application for a Master Development Plan Amendment was duly filed
on January 18th, 2024 by Steve Kohner to allow for 49 single family lots, 74 townhome units, and
changes to the roadways as well as open spaces in the Starlight Ridge Estates Special Development
within the Wagon Wheel area; and
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County:
APN # 212-05-007N, 212-05-007P, 212-05-007Q, 212-05-007R, 212-05-007S, Township 09
North, Range 22 East, Section 08 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian; and
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on July 18th, 2024; and
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that
the Master Development Plan Amendment is not consistent with the public health, safety and
general welfare and should be denied; and
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby deny
the Master Development Plan Amendment.
DENIED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas and
______ nays on this 27th day of August 2024.

Planning and Zoning Commission 
Navajo County Public Works 
100 West Public Works Drive 
Holbrook, AZ 86025 
Meeting Date June 20, 2024, 6:00 pm 
MDP-A 24-001 Starlight Ridge Estates Unit III, District V 
Commissioners: 
We are the owners of Lot 45 in Starlight Ridge Estates Unit 1. Our property is one of two that is currently 
vacant on Wagon Wheel Lane directly east of Rim Road and parcel APN# 212-05-007Q. We bought the 
property approximately a year ago with the intention of building our dream vacation getaway house on the 
property. Since we have encountered some health challenges, we currently have the lot again for sale, but we 
are hoping to overcome those challenges and start the build in the future. 
We are writing to ask you to deny the request before you to amend the Master Plan for Starlight Ridge Estates 
Unit III, District V. We object to the amendment for these reasons: 
1. Increase in traffic 
2. Inadequate drainage considerations 
3. Availability of water for additional homes in the area 
4. Disruption of scenic views 
 
1.Increase in traffic 
Although the amendment introduces a new entrance to Unit III, such entrance requires traffic to enter Starlight 
Ridge Estates via Wagon Wheel Lane, currently a loop through the subdivision. Plans to extend Rim Road 
require approval from the White Mountain Apache Tribe and such approval may require years to complete. 
Construction traffic and homeowner traffic to the new Unit III will contribute to dust and noise pollution in the 
area. The movement of road building vehicles and utility expansion vehicles on Wagon Wheel Lane and onto 
Highway 260 will be onerous for current residents. 
 
2. Inadequate Drainage 
The amended plan routes drainage from Unit III in two places across Wagon Wheel Lane to a detention pond. 
The area has considerable water runoff already during winter melt and monsoon rains, and our lot would sit in 
the middle of the two drainage points across Wagon Wheel Lane. 
3. Availability of water 
The developer has received certification from the City of Show Low to provide water to the area, but drought 
conditions continue in Arizona and water supply may be severely limited in the future, regardless of promises 
made to provide it.

4. Disruption of scenic views 
The amendment to the master plan comprises single family homes that conform to the Starlight Ridge 
Homeowners’ Association architectural guidelines. Noticeably the minimum lot sizes are considerably smaller 
in the amended plan. Further, while the open space in the proposed Unit III is 28%, that open space is behind 
the proposed housing and thus will not be available for enjoyment by the current community. 
Please reject the amendment to the master plan MDP-A 24-001 for Starlight Ridge Estates Unit III because of 
the objections we have listed above.  
Sincerely, 
Mary & Tim Drago 
Lot 45 Starlight Ridge Estates, Unit I

Re: Agenda Item MDP-A 24-001 Starlight Ridge Estates Unit #3 District V 
 
My name is Ronald Monette and I have lived at 6558 Wagon Wheel Lane for 33 years. During 
that time I have witnessed much change to the neighborhood. Twenty years ago Wagon Wheel 
Lane was a quiet, dead end street until the land that is now Starlight Ridge Estates was 
developed. At the time there was significant push back from the local neighborhood on the 
development; however, assured that most of the units would be reserved for single family lots 
and not wanting to stand in the way of progress, Navajo Country approved the development.  
 
Fast forward to today, and the developers want to change that deal to permit more density and 
to increase their profits. Here’s why I believe the current permitted application of the land should 
remain the same: 
 
1. The infrastructure built by these developers has not withstood the test of time and has 
not been properly repaired or replaced. Adding more density to this neighborhood will 
only exacerbate the current issues. See photos below for examples of the current road 
conditions. Photos taken 6/19/24. 
 
 
2. Adding more residences will place an extra burden on already thin county services. 
a. The added revenue from additional property taxes will not offset the costs 
incurred by the county for these added residences. Property taxes only account 
for 19% of the county’s budget. (Payson Roundup, 2024)

<!-- page 321 -->
3. The land has already been approved for single family residences and the developers still 
stand to make a good profit if they develop what they are currently permitted for.  
 
While many of us don’t want the added density to our neighborhood, there are other valid 
reasons for the county to consider denying this zoning change.  
ARS 11-811,  Zoning ordinance; zoning districts; definitions 
A. Pursuant to this article, the board of supervisors may adopt a zoning ordinance 
in order to conserve and promote the public health, safety, convenience and 
general welfare.  The zoning ordinance and all rezonings and zoning 
regulations amendments adopted under this article shall be consistent with and 
conform to the adopted comprehensive plan.  In addition to the other matters 
that are required or authorized under this section and article 1 of this chapter, 
the zoning ordinance: C. In accordance with article II, sections 1 and 2, 
Constitution of Arizona, the board shall consider the individual property rights 
and personal liberties of the residents of the county before adopting any zoning 
ordinance. 
11-814. Rezoning; conditional zoning change; notice; hearing; citizen review; 
definition 
E. If twenty per cent of the owners of property by area and number within the 
zoning area file a protest to the proposed rezoning, the change shall not be 
made except by a three-fourths vote of all members of the board for those 
counties with five or more supervisors.  If any members of the board are unable 
to vote on the question because of a conflict of interest, the required number of 
votes for the passage of the question is three-fourths of the remaining 
membership of the board for those counties with five or more supervisors, 
except that the required number of votes in no event shall be less than a 
majority of the full membership of the board.  In calculating the owners by 
area, only that portion of a lot or parcel of record situated within three hundred 
feet of the property to be rezoned shall be included.  In calculating the owners 
by number or area, county property and public rights-of-way shall not be 
included. 
 
11-832. Appeals of county actions; dedication or exaction; excessive reduction in 
property value; burden of proof; attorney fees; compliance with court decisions

<!-- page 322 -->
I. A county or an agency or instrumentality of a county shall comply with the United 
States Supreme Court cases of Dolan v. City of Tigard, 512 U.S. 374 (1994), Nollan 
v. California Coastal Commission, 483 U.S. 825 (1987), Lucas v. South Carolina 
Coastal Council, 505 U.S. 1003 (1992), First English Evangelical Lutheran Church v. 
County of Los Angeles, 482 U.S. 304 (1987), Palazzolo v. Rhode Island, 533 U.S. 
606 (2001), Tahoe-Sierra Preservation Council, Inc. v. Tahoe Regional Planning 
Agency, 535 U.S. 320 (2002) and Arizona and federal appellate court decisions that 
are binding on Arizona counties interpreting or applying those cases. 
 
 
I hope that you will consider the appeals of me and my neighbors and take into consideration 
that the land is already approved for a large, profitable development. 
 
Sincerely,

From: Kathleen O'Hanian <azfaf14@gmail.com>

24-001 Starlight Ridge Estates Unit III, District

Caution: This email originated from outside of Navajo County. 
RE: Planning and Zoning Commission meeting June 20th at 6:00 PM. 
Agenda Item: MDP-A 24-001 Starlight Ridge Estates Unit III, District V 
Dear Navajo County Development Services Department: 
My husband Oscar and I, Kathleen O’hanian own a home in Starlight Ridge Estates, Lot 43 on the corner 
of Wagon Wheel Lane and Starlight Ridge. The proposed change in zoning is less than 300 feet of our 
property. We would like to voice our concern about the proposed plan for the 53 acres of forest land 
directly across the street. Our concerns are as follows: 
 The homes that are currently in the neighborhood are on approximately .50 acre lots which gives 
each home ample space between homes and greatly increases the value of each property. It also 
protects the wildlife habitat and gives the wildlife plenty of space to roam. Having lot sizes less 
than that for several of the single family proposed home sites goes against the original 
neighborhood plan and in our opinion devalues our property. We chose this neighborhood mainly 
for the size of lots and the overall atmosphere that provides.  
 Although a traffic study was conducted, the exit main exit point for the proposed 123 homes (49 
single family homes and 74 townhomes) is very close to our property. The added 123+ (possibly 
over 200) cars coming and going from that area will greatly affect the current quietness of the 
neighborhood and decrease our property value.  
 At times currently it takes several minutes to enter White Mountain Blvd using either Wagon 
Wheel Lane or Webb Drive. We understand that a traffic study was performed and it may have 
concluded that it was favorable but 123+ additional vehicles on a daily basis will cause a long 
lineup of vehicles trying to enter WMB safely. Cars exiting either way will be required to pass by 
our property effecting the noise level in our home. It will definitely change the atmosphere of the 
entire neighborhood. 
 Water runoff is a huge concern for us. If lots are small and that leaves little room for proper water 
absorption, much of the proposed drainage still runs towards our property. We have witnessed on 
many occasions during the summer months, the drainage ditch in front of our property rising to 
 Some people who received this message don't often get email from azfaf14@gmail.com. Learn why this is important

full levels. Any additional water in this culvert has the potential to overflow onto our property and 
cause damage. 
With many concerns, we ask that the proposed zoning not be approved and the original

integrity of the Starlight Ridge Estates neighborhood and protect the current homeowners 
value of their properties and homes. 
Thank you.

Board of Supervisors Regular 10. a.        
Meeting Date: 08/27/2024  
Title: IGA between Navajo County and the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside
Submitted For: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer 
Submitted By: William Flake, Assistant County Engineer
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Will Flake
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of an Intergovernmental Agreement between Navajo County and the Town of
Pinetop-Lakeside to allow for a loan in the amount of $610,000 for the construction of a portion of Porter
Mountain Road, to be reimbursed back to Navajo county no later than March 31, 2025.
Background:
The Town of Pinetop-Lakeside has awarded a contract to Perkins Cinders, Inc. to construct the Porter
Mountain Road Improvements Project and the Blue Ridge Unified School District and was awarded
Adjacent Way Funds in the amount of $610,000 to be paid to the Contractor for the improvements
(approved by Resolution No. 24-1707 at the Town Council meeting on April 18, 2024). However, the
Adjacent Way funding will not be distributed until February 2025. Therefore, if approved, the County
will provide the funds to the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside for an amount of $610,000, and the Town has
agreed to reimburse the County the full loan amount no later than March 31, 2025. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside
(Town) to allow a loan in the amount of $610,000 for the construction of Porter Mountain Road, which
will be reimbursed back to Navajo County by the Town no later than March 31, 2025.
Fiscal Impact

Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: $610,000
